#3219. Report: Apple captured half of global smartphone revenue in Q3

According to a new report from Counterpoint Research, Apple just recorded its highest-ever global smartphone revenue for a third calendar quarter. Here are the details.

In a report released earlier this week, Counterpoint Research mapped out the global smartphone market’s performance during the third quarter, which saw Apple capture its highest-ever share of industry revenue for the period.

Samsung, which came in second, accounted for 19% of global smartphone revenue, followed by Xiaomi at 6%, OPPO at 5%, and vivo at 4%.

In another record, Counterpoint says Apple saw the iPhone average selling price (ASP) hit an all-time-high of $1,031, up 19% year-over-year.

Counterpoint’s Research Director Tarun Pathak explained that the iPhone 18 Pro series contributed to Apple’s ASP growth, despite launching toward the end of the third calendar quarter:

“Apple remained the most resilient major brand in Q3 2026, with shipments nearly flat YoY. Apple has the pricing power to absorb rising memory costs and pass them on to consumers with minimal impact on demand. At the same time, ASP growth was supported by higher launch prices for the iPhone 18 Pro series, the absence of the base iPhone 18 from the launch and price increases across older models in September. Together, these factors helped Apple deliver strong revenue growth in an otherwise challenging market.”

Finally, the report noted that while Samsung still led the market in shipment share, its lead over Apple narrowed slightly, with the companies accounting for 22% and 21% of global smartphone shipments, respectively.

Experts explain how this hidden iPhone setting can improve your sleep

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

I write about sleep for a living, but I've been secretly locked in a losing battle against my doomscrolling habits. I'd constantly push bedtime later to watch a few more reels or reply to a couple more messages — and my nocturnal phone habit meant that getting out of bed each morning was becoming a chore.

That was until I stumbled on a hidden feature in my Apple Health App. Now I'm far more disciplined when it comes to using my phone around bedtime, making a conscious effort to give myself time to truly relax in the evening and time to ease into the day come morning.

Below, I dig into how I altered my iPhone settings to help me get to sleep on time and wake up with tons more energy. Plus, Dr. Shelby Harris, a board-certified behavioral sleep medicine specialist and psychologist, explains why enabling this feature works and why it's so important to restrict phone use around bedtime for better sleep.

During my time as a sleep tech tester, I've tried my fair share of sleep gadgets. I'll forever rave about the top-rated sunrise alarm clocks and don't know where I'd be without by trusty Oura Ring to help track my rest. But, understandably, I'm yet to find anything that can physically restrain me from using my phone in bed.

"The biggest problem with your phone at night isn’t the screen itself," explains Dr. Harris, who has over 20 years' experience in sleep medicine. "It’s what’s on it. Social media, videos, and games are designed to keep you hooked, with endless feeds and autoplay that make it easy to lose track of time. Before you know it, “just a few minutes” has turned into an hour of lost sleep."

Our willpower is no match for Meta, basically. That's why it's so important nowadays to have measures and discipline in place to stop the endless scrolling when you should be sleeping.

I recommend everybody turns on Sleep Schedule in the Apple Health app. This feature allows you to set a recurring bedtime and wake-up routine. During sleep time, your phone will automatically shift into Sleep Focus mode where notifications and calls are silenced, your lock screen simplifies and phone screens dims.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

Without your phone constantly buzzing at your bedside, it's less tempting to pick up and even gives you the chance to forget about your constant notification feed while you drift off to sleep.

Depending on the Wind Down period you set, you'll get a notification telling you to prioritise your sleep. I get this 15 minutes before my desired bedtime, though I'm keen to push this earlier after hearing Dr. Harris' advice.

The Diplomate in Behavioral Sleep Medicine says: "The best approach is to put your phone away, ideally outside the bedroom, 30 to 60 minutes before bed and replace scrolling with something calming, like reading a book or stretching."

"The iPhone’s sleep tools help turn good intentions into habits," says Dr. Harris. To set up your personalised sleep schedule, head to the Apple Health app (that pink heart in a white square). Find the Sleep section and scroll down to 'Your Schedule'. Open 'Full Schedule & Options'.

Here you can toggle on Sleep Schedule and link it to your phone's Sleep Focus setting.

There's the option to customise your schedule for different days of the week. However, experts recommend sticking to the same sleep and wake times seven nights a week as this helps sync your circadian rhythm (more on this below).

You can also set a Wind Down period between 15 minutes and three hours. During this time, your phone shifts into Sleep mode. Health will notify you when Wind Down begins, encouraging you to transition from scrolling to rest.

For full peace of mind if you have caring responsibilities, you can select certain contacts to break through the notification block. This means you won't block out any important phone calls through the night.

Sleeping at consistent times from night to night is quickly becoming the most common sleep advice dished out by experts.

Recent science – including a major study by Vitality Research Institute – indicates the 7:1 sleep rule is the bedrock of quality rest. The rule stipulates getting seven hours of sleep every single night keeping your bedtime within in a one hour window is the secret to sleeping well, waking up energized, and indeed living up to four years longer.

Dr. Harris, who specialises in circadian rhythm disorders, explains: "Your body runs on an internal clock that controls when you feel sleepy and when you feel alert. When you go to bed and wake up at the same time every day, that clock learns to anticipate sleep, so you fall asleep faster, sleep more deeply, and wake up feeling more refreshed."

Sticking to a consistent sleep schedule helps sync your circadian rhythm. When this is in tune, your body will naturally release sleep-inducing hormones like melatonin around bedtime and suppress them come morning so you wake up without lingering grogginess (scientifically named sleep inertia).

Whereas "Irregular schedules, especially sleeping in on weekends, throw that rhythm off and leave you feeling jet-lagged without ever leaving home," adds Dr. Harris.

With a well regulated circadian cycle, you should release the right amount of melatonin through the night to keep you in a healthy sleep cycle, so you get good quality sleep and wake up feeling refreshed.

As your circadian rhythm also controls hormones that promote alertness (cortisol and serotonin), you should also experience more stable energy levels through the day.

Ultimately, the New York-based sleep psychologist advises, "If you focus on one thing, make it a consistent wake-up time. That’s the anchor that keeps everything else on track" — and Apple's Sleep Schedule feature can help you do just that, especially if you're a stickler for late night scrolling.

A two-in-one sunrise alarm clock and sleep tracker, the Hatch Sleep Clock is the ultimate gadget for keeping your sleep in check. Sunrise and sunset simulations help sync your body clock while gentle sounds lull you in and out of sleep – all for $249.99 plus a $4.99/month subscription fee to access all features.

Vetted the best sleep tracker around by our sleep tech reviewers, the Oura Ring 5 keeps track of the exact time you fall asleep and wake up, and helps coach you towards a consistent schedule. There's six colorways from gold to rose gold and silver and eight sizing options. Prices range from $399 to $499.

If it's a snoring partner getting in the way of your consistent sleep routine, the Loop Queit 2 Ear Plugs are a dream. Sitting subtly in your ears, they muffle sound without causing discomfort. Our reviewer, a chronically light sleeper, says: "The Loop Quiet 2 are ideal for dropping of peacefully without you missing your alarm in the morning." Prices start at $24.95 and they can be reused night after night.

#3217. The Swift Observatory Dropped Back to Earth This Week. Here’s What to Know

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

NASA believes most of the space telescope burned up in the atmosphere, but some fragments may have made their way down to Earth.

The Neil Gehrels Swift Observatory, formerly known as the Swift Gamma-Ray Burst Explorer, has reached the end of its journey. Launched into orbit in 2004, the space telescope completed an extraordinary 22-year mission when it plunged back into Earth’s atmosphere on Wednesday.

“Swift delivered an incredible, lasting return on investment for the nation, designed for a two-year primary mission but productive for more than 10 times as long,” Shawn Domagal-Goldman, director of the Astrophysics Division at NASA, said in a statement last month.

NASA says the telescope hit the atmosphere Wednesday at 7:39 am ET, just four hours after the space agency predicted.

The space telescope entered orbit over the North Sumatra region of Indonesia, just west of Malaysia. NASA says that most of the space telescope likely burned up upon reentry but that some components may have survived.

In preparation for Swift’s reentry, NASA shut down operations in late October after conducting its final observation on Oct. 20. It was orbiting at just 171 miles above Earth’s surface at that point, and the increased atmospheric drag prevented Swift from doing any further work.

It was a bittersweet moment for those who worked on the project.

“It is a sad time at the Penn State Mission Operations Center, as we have carefully tended Swift to control its science operations, its health and safety, and responded to anomalies day or night, every day for 22 years,” John Nousek, director of mission operations for Swift and professor of astronomy and astrophysics at Penn State, said in an email to CNET.

Swift’s initial mission helped advance science’s understanding of gamma-ray bursts and their role in phenomena such as the birth of black holes and the history of the early universe.

That initial mission ended in 2004, but the observatory was doing so well that NASA extended its operations to continue collecting data. Its name changed to the Neil Gehrels Swift Observatory in 2018, in honor of pioneering NASA astrophysicist Neil Gehrels, who served as the agency’s chief of the Astroparticle Physics Laboratory from 1995 until his death in 2017.

The space telescope was designed to help solve the mystery of gamma-ray bursts by rapidly detecting them, determining their sources and then further analyzing them for study back on Earth. It also enabled unique sampling to observe newly discovered objects only minutes after their light reached Earth, Nousek said.

The volume of science research conducted using Swift’s data is difficult to match. According to NASA, Swift scanned over 2,000 gamma-ray bursts, took 1.5 million images from its ultraviolet/optical telescope and sent home 12 TB of data. That data is showcased in an estimated 7,000 research papers, which averages out to just under one study per day over its 22-year run.

“We hope Swift has set the standard for time domain astrophysics,” Nousek said.

The space telescope also helped pioneer many policies that ultimately shaped those discoveries, such as making data immediately accessible and providing over 1,000 guest investigators with the opportunity to use Swift throughout its long life.

It’s only a stroke of bad luck that kept the observatory from floating for even longer. NASA attempted a rescue of Swift over the summer of 2026. However, after a series of engineering difficulties and control issues, the attempt was abandoned, sealing Swift’s fate. NASA turned the instruments back on and kept recording data as long as possible.

#3215. Robinhood Made $173 Million From Prediction Markets Last Quarter, More Than From Crypto. Can That Carry the Stock?

Prediction markets have quickly become one of Robinhood's most important sales drivers.

Robinhood Markets (HOOD -10.32%) reported its third-quarter results after the market closed on Oct. 27, and performance in the period didn't thrill investors. The company reported non-GAAP (adjusted) earnings per share of $0.51 on sales of $1.42 billion, missing the average analyst estimate of earnings per share of $0.56 on sales of $1.43 billion. While the company's revenue was up 12% year over year to a new record high, earnings per share fell 3% from the prior-year period.

The market wasn't thrilled with the quarterly print, but there were some bright spots. Notably, sales from the company's prediction market ventures increased more than sixfold annually to reach $173 million. The big growth helped offset a substantial decline for the company's cryptocurrency segment, which saw sales decline 55% year over year to reach $120 million. Are event contracts now central to Robinhood's growth story?

The rise of prediction markets has occurred at an incredible pace, with service providers including Kalshi and Polymarket rapidly growing their user bases and commanding billion-dollar market capitalizations. Event contracts allow investors to bet on real-world outcomes in spheres including politics, awards show results, sports, and even the weather -- and participants can sell in and out of positions based on current market rates in much the same way that they would buy and sell stocks.

The popularity of prediction markets is exploding, and Robinhood's massive category revenue growth reflects how lucrative it can be to provide services in this category. Given the expansion trajectory for the company's event contracts segment, Robinhood's prediction market business looks poised to achieve an annual revenue run rate of more than $1 billion in the near future -- and it could still have room for big growth from there.

With the company's cryptocurrency segment seeing big sales declines, strong performance for the event contracts business is helping to smooth out performance and facilitate continued overall revenue growth for the overall business. On the other hand, there are still some significant risks associated with the rapidly ascendant prediction-market business.

The prediction market industry is still in its "Wild West" phase, and there's a real possibility the category will face a wave of new regulations in the not-too-distant future. Shifting regulatory dynamics could cause growth in the category to slow substantially or introduce higher expenses for operators, and there's even a risk that current operators of event contracts and adjacent services could wind up being hit with sizable fines at some point down the road.

Given recent trends in financialization, regulators may have trouble getting the toothpaste back in the tube with prediction markets -- but investors should understand that disruption risks exist for Robinhood's hot new business category. While the introduction of Robinhood's event-contracts business has been an undeniable boon to sales and earnings and could help carry the stock over the long term, there's still some uncertainty on that front.

Keith Noonan has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool recommends Robinhood Markets. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

#3214. Apple expected to bid for F1 streaming rights in more countries, says Formula 1 CEO

On the latest earnings call for F1’s parent company Liberty Media, the CEO of Formula 1 Stefano Domenicali said that both parties are happy with the US deal, and he expects that Apple will bid for rights in more countries as the opportunities become available.

However, it seems the league is open to striking more deals with Apple in certain markets.

On the earnings call, in response to a question from an investor about Apple’s interest, Domenicali said “this is part of our discussion, if there are opportunities that can be in other markets, they will do it … they’re going to do it as soon as these opportunities will be open for them”.

Liberty Media CEO Derek Chang said they continue to think about what new innovation they can bring to the product side, and noted that “Apple as a potential bidder in other markets is helpful” for their business.

As a reminder, all Formula 1 races are available to watch live and on-demand with an Apple TV subscription, in the United States. The company recently launched a Dolby Atmos audio track for its F1 streams, as well as expanded support for Multiview on more platforms, including iPhone Duo.

#3213. Did the AI bubble just pop? $2 trillion reality check hits Nvidia, Meta & SpaceX as Wall Street panics, signaling a potential end to RAM crisis in 2027

If you want to know what it looks like when a trillion-dollar tech bubble violently collides with physical reality, just look at yesterday. $2.5 trillion in tech valuations were wiped off the board as Wall Street panicked about AI’s leverage — punishing tech giants for incinerating cash on infrastructure that isn’t generating immediate profit.

Simultaneously, as we saw on Wednesday, the American voter rejected data center expansions in 40 out of 41 local ballot measures. The hardware hoarders are losing their grip, and the panic is palpable. The era of scrutiny is here, and I want to show you two possible timelines of where this could go.

But first, we have to go back over a couple things.

When faced with this stock crash, AMD CEO Lisa Su said, “We are a data center first company.” While I’d love to throw my hands up and get angry at this, I have to turn the BS detector on myself and address a brutal truth: she’s right. This market crash is a sentiment correction, not an overnight fab cancellation.

The big three memory foundries — SK Hynix, Samsung and Micron — operate on long-term agreements. 2026-2027 is already locked in at sky-high prices, and on top of that, enterprise AI accelerators consume roughly 3x the silicon wafers of standard consumer DDR5 memory.

As long as those massive AI server chips are shipping, conventional RAM capacity remains deeply suppressed.

So, if Wall Street is slashing valuations and data center permits are being blocked, why aren’t memory foundries pivoting back to consumer PC hardware right now?

Sign up to the Tom's AI Guide weekly newsletter summing up all the biggest AI news you need to know. Plus, analysis from our AI editors and tips on how to use the latest AI tools!

Hyperscalers are also starving for memory, and receive around 60-70% of what they ask for. If Microsoft and Meta get spooked by the market and pull back their massive order growth by 10% or even 15%, it wouldn’t trigger a supply chain pivot. It just means the foundries are a little less overwhelmed. Demand still vastly exceeds total factory capacity.

If foundries are still frantically trying to fulfill enterprise contracts, they have zero financial incentive to redirect back to consumer memory.

For them to actually panic and pivot, hyperscaler orders have to crater. A simple market correction like this won’t do it. But a sweeping wave of municipal zoning bans? That changes the math entirely.

Top of the Democratic agenda on day 1 needs to be a pause on advanced AI development, regulation of this out of control industry, and a ban on artificial superintelligence.November 4, 2026

If tech giants are legally barred from breaking ground on new server farms, they are forced to cancel their existing silicon orders because they physically have nowhere to plug the chips in.

We are not getting a massive surplus of GPUs and memory kits tomorrow morning. But the midterms and the stock market correction did put a concrete ceiling on the shortage. So let me take a stab at how I think this is going to unfold.

The stock rout has cracked the leveraged AI market for now, but contract prices remain firm. There is zero immediate relief, so don’t expect some banger Black Friday deals on RTX 5090s.

Memory shortages will hit their tightest consumer squeeze through early 2027. PC makers will fiercely defend a ceiling of 16GB-24GB on premium laptops and handhelds. If you’re hoping for 32GB in sub-$1,500 machines, shelve that hope through most of next year.

As hyperscalers face sustained stock and regulatory pressure from the midterm fallout, corporate boards will force company CFOs to look at their data center infrastructure plans again.

Instead of writing blank checks for server racks, the pace of subsequent purchase orders will slow. And for you reading this, I envision that PC hardware prices will finally stop climbing and plateau.

PC builders (Apple, Dell, Asus, Lenovo, etc) will gain massive leverage during mid-year procurement negotiations — putting an end to double-digit quarter-on-quarter price hikes.

A typical memory wafer run takes 3-4 months. Once the AI server orders officially ease in Q2, foundries will finally redirect their wafers back to consumer products. This is when the true price relief begins.

Standard desktop DDR5 kits will experience price drops, and holiday 2027 laptops will finally start normalizing 32GB unified configurations without severe margin penalties.

We aren’t completely out of the woods, and we still have to survive a brutal winter. But in my mind, those 2028 doom-and-gloom shortage predictions are officially dead.

The hyperscalers just hit a political and financial ceiling, which may lead to a peak in memory pricing this spring rather than running unchecked into next year.

Hold off on your PC upgrades — the local AI renaissance could be scheduled for the second half of 2027.

#3211. Want an AI job? Deployment engineers are earning executive-level pay – up to $1.45M a year

AI has ushered in new jobs, and some of them are paying handsomely, according to a new study.

AI engineers are making well over six figures in salary — and plenty more in bonuses and equity, according to data from a new report by executive search firm Christian & Timbers.

Enterprises now pay “elite” AI deployment engineers between $586,000 and $1.45 million annually in total compensation, with senior leaders in the field earning up to $2.25 million, according to the report. This figure approaches the median compensation of executive officers of public companies, the authors note.

Also: ‘So perfect, they are literally identical’ – Your AI-polished resumes are recruiters’ new top stressor

Christian & Timbers is an AI executive search and leadership advisory firm, recruiting CEOs, board members, technology executives, and AI engineers for enterprises. The new report draws on more than 550 interviews with engineers, deployment leaders, and executives conducted from January through June 2026, along with closed and current open offers. The compensation bands are Christian & Timbers’ estimates and represent ranges rather than statistical averages.

“Enterprise CEOs are under pressure to deliver returns for their AI investments. The most important decision is how to build the team that delivers on that goal,” said Jeff Christian, founder and CEO of Christian & Timbers, in a press release.

Also: What workers are really using AI for in 2026 – and what they aren’t

The report noted that the staggering salary range includes base salary, bonus, and annualized equity. Christian & Timbers said it defines an “elite” forward deployment engineer (FDE) as “a senior AI-native deployment builder, typically with 7 to 15 years of experience, including at least two years building and deploying agentic automated workflows that deliver measurable ROI with a documented enterprise impact of $10M or more,” who has also led a team of other forward deployment engineers.

Also: 50% of companies generate value from AI now – here’s how

The large salary ranges reflect increasing enterprise investments in AI talent and employee development. Microsoft, AWS, and OpenAI have announced more than $7.5 billion in corporate and partner investment in AI deployment over the past six months, Christian & Timbers noted in the release.

Also: Nearly 70% of workers use AI regularly now – but many get no time to upskill

There’s an increasing need to fill roles such as Senior Principal AI Engineer, AI Solutions Engineer, and Head of Deployment — but there is a limited pool of qualified candidates. The firm estimates that only 2,000 of the most senior deployment leaders exist in the US market, according to a previous Christian & Timbers study, which does not meet demand — so companies are competing for the same small group of professionals, hence the increasing salaries.

While the report underscores lucrative yields for certain engineering roles, the firm said that public job titles make comparisons more challenging.

“Christian & Timbers’ elite category spans several titles, while advertised FDE roles cover a broader range of experience and responsibilities. Experienced deployment engineers may be recruited directly or promoted into the work under titles such as staff software engineer or principal AI engineer,” the study said.

Plus, equity and bonus make up 52% to 66% of senior engineer pay, and most job postings don’t include that value in the salary, the report noted.

#3210. Reformation: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

Returning customers nearly double their spend in year two, a pattern that matters more to long-term holders than any single quarter's sales.

On its third quarter of 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 5, 2026, Reformation (REF -8.47%) announced full-year net revenue growth is expected to exceed 20%. The retailer scales direct-to-consumer sales made directly to shoppers without third-party retailers while expanding profitability through agile manufacturing. Three things management said stand out.

Reformation responds to shifting fashion preferences within weeks rather than planning inventory months in advance. This speed allows the business to test styles in small batches and quickly scale top sellers.

Expanding physical locations across major markets drives online traffic while generating store-level profitability. Physical retail introduces new shoppers to the brand at lower customer acquisition costs.

Active customers expanded 23% year over year to 1.25 million shoppers. While new shoppers spend less during their first year, returning customer cohorts double their annual purchases over time.

Net revenue momentum carried directly into the fourth quarter as third-quarter results exceeded internal targets. Reformation relies on flexible manufacturing and targeted store expansion, while executive teams track pricing discipline and average unit retail, the average selling price of merchandise.

International store expansion and selective wholesale partnerships offer clear long-term growth avenues. High full-price sell-through and disciplined cost control protect underlying margins as the business scales.

#3209. Report: iPad shipments drop 27.5% as global tablet market plunges in Q3

Despite global iPad shipments dropping 27.5% year-over-year in the third calendar quarter, Apple maintained its lead in the tablet market, according to a new report released today. Here are the details.

According to a new report published by Omdia (via MacMagazine), Apple shipped 11,5 million iPads during Q3 2026, accounting for 36% of all shipments.

Compared to Q3 2025, that was a 27.5% drop in shipments, and a 2.2 percentage-point decline in market share.

For context, the global tablet market saw a 23% year-over-year drop, with total shipments going from 41.8 million to 32.2 million units. Omdia noted this was the lowest level in nearly three years.

As for Apple’s competitors, Samsung remained in second place with 16.9% market share, followed by Lenovo with 12.7%, Xiaomi with 7.3%, and Huawei with 5.7%.

When it comes to shipment growth, Lenovo was the only company among the top five to post a positive result, up 11.9% year over year. Huawei saw the steepest decline at 43%, followed by Apple at 27.5%, Samsung at 21.7%, and Xiaomi at 10.6%.

Commenting on the results, Omdia Research Manager Himani Mukka said supply constraints are “limiting vendors’ ability to deliver meaningful specification upgrades, leaving fewer compelling differences between new models and their predecessors.”

“As a result,” she added, “consumers are increasingly expected to delay purchases until more substantial improvements become available.”

#3207. Meet the Dividend King Stock That Yields Nearly Quadruple the Nasdaq-100. Here's Why It's a Buy in November.

Coca-Cola's earnings easily support dividends.

It's certainly an acceptable strategy to invest in growth stocks. However, you can balance growth investing with dividend-paying stocks. The latter provide income and tend to have less volatility than high-growth stocks.

The Nasdaq-100 has a strong technology weighting. Investors have favored these stocks, and the index gained 11.8% this year, through Nov. 2. But that's almost entirely from appreciation since the index has just a 0.6% dividend yield.

Coca-Cola's (KO -0.07%) stock yields nearly quadruple the Nasdaq-100. Here's why dividend-seeking investors should add the stock to their portfolio.

Investors should take notice and applaud a company that raises dividends annually for decades. That's the case with Coca-Cola, which has increased payments for 64 straight years.

It's incredible to think about all the things that have happened (e.g., wars, recessions, stagflation, the financial crisis, and the pandemic) during those years. Yet Coca-Cola has managed to raise dividends each and every year no matter what events were transpiring.

In fact, companies that have raised dividends for at least 50 straight years belong to a special group, called Dividend Kings.

Earlier this year, the board of directors approved a 4% increase in the quarterly rate to $0.53 per share. At the new rate, Coca-Cola's stock yields 2.3%.

As noted, that's much higher than the Nasdaq-100's dividend yield. It's also more than double the popular S&P 500 index, which yields 1.1%.

The company's payouts don't appear to be in danger of getting cut, either. Coca-Cola has a payout ratio (dividends divided by earnings) of 62%.

While Coca-Cola, selling products in more than 200 countries, no longer has huge growth potential, it has been steadily growing sales and earnings. That's an important consideration for investors looking for an attractive total return.

Generally, consumers have been struggling with higher costs, but they're not skimping on Coca-Cola products. And having a global presence helps offset weakness in specific regions.

The company's third-quarter sales, adjusted to remove foreign-currency translations and acquisitions/divestitures, grew 7% year over year. Importantly, virtually all of the growth came from higher volume rather than merely increasing prices. The higher sales helped push earnings higher. Coca-Cola's quarterly adjusted earnings per share increased 9%.

Management expects at least 5% and 9% increases in sales and earnings per share, respectively, for the year.

Coca-Cola, in existence since 1886, may not provide the kind of upside that a hot tech company does, but it will provide ever-growing dividends and steady growth.

For investors looking to reduce volatility and earn income, Coca-Cola certainly fits the bill.

Lawrence Rothman, CFA has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

#3206. Researchers asked people to use AI chatbots for election info – results were mixed

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Chatbots can skew election-related information and act as an intervening layer in a voter's search.

Most search tools now put a layer of AI between you and the information you’re looking for, especially Google.

Also: Tired of AI Overviews? I found 9 Google Search alternatives

In a new study, Yale researchers found that a third of users asked a chatbot to help them pick a candidate. Grok often recommended candidates on its own without any information from users, while ChatGPT recommended candidates most often overall, and twice as much as Claude.

The researchers asked nearly 2,500 people in 35 states with Senate races to query ChatGPT, Claude, Grok, or Gemini about the election. Participants in the study, which ran for a few weeks in October leading up to the Nov. 3 midterm elections, were adults who already use chatbots weekly.

Most users asked chatbots for basic facts about the election, including who was on the ballot. But 36% asked a chatbot to help them pick a candidate to vote for, often by describing their priorities and asking the chatbot to assess which candidates aligned with them.

The former query has slightly less room for error, because the internet has a ton of data on the basic facts of an election, such as who is running where. Chatbots interpreting candidate positions, on the other hand, is a bit more dicey, and relies significantly on the sources a chatbot uses (a statement from a candidate’s own website, for example, is more reliable than a social media clip of an opponent characterizing them in a certain way).

Also: How to keep your chatbot conversations as private as possible

In one example, a user asked ChatGPT to give them the “pros and cons” of each candidate in Virginia, an inherently subjective question.

When it came to recommending candidates, chatbots varied widely.

“When users asked who to vote for but had not yet said anything about themselves, only Grok regularly recommended a candidate, in 14% of chats,” the researchers wrote. “Once users shared their views, ChatGPT recommended a candidate in 79% of chats and Claude in 40%, with Grok and Gemini in between.”

Pew Research found that 42% of US adults surveyed use chatbots to search for information. That’s a broad umbrella that can include election information — but Pew also found that only 8% of US adults reported using chatbots to find election-related information in this year’s election.

What those users are looking for matters, though. Pew found that of that group, 76% were inquiring about where candidates stand on certain issues, and 71% were fact-checking information they’d heard about candidates or issues.

Also: OpenAI will merge ChatGPT’s chat and work tabs

Given that chatbots are still not reliable summarizers, the nature of these questions poses a misinformation risk if users don’t also check a chatbot’s linked sources. An overwhelming majority of users don’t, at least when it comes to Google’s AI Overviews. At least one study found AI platforms gave users “outdated or inaccurate” information that could mislead voters in some cases by missing a deadline or failing to mention a step about voting logistics.

This election cycle was particularly plagued by AI-generated deepfakes and misinformation as tools have become much more sophisticated since the 2024 election. OpenAI and Anthropic reportedly set up election “war rooms” to monitor misuse and information accuracy, according to exclusive reporting by Politico and Semafor.

While an August study from the Brennan Center for Justice found that chatbots did push back surprisingly well against certain falsehoods related to election disinformation campaigns, it also found that AI models easily generated “misleading election-related images and video” when asked.

“Many of their safeguards for preventing this were ineffective, inconsistently applied, or functionally absent,” noted the study, which tested ChatGPT, Gemini, Grok, Meta AI, Runway, and Flux.2.

Also: 50 AI agents tried to build a thriving Minecraft civilization – it didn’t end well

Apart from these factors, however, there is a broader theme in AI use that is equally consequential for civic engagement: cognitive offloading. The political media landscape can already be dense and hard to parse for a voter trying to stay informed. Layering a chatbot — which has opaque training data sets, filters, and other settings determined at the discretion of AI labs — on top of that information search process can further muddy the waters while appearing like a convenient and neutral summarizing tool.

While some studies test how AI chatbots respond to election questions by using standardized questions, this approach let users themselves engage with chatbots as they normally would. While the Yale researchers’ queries and standardized study queries show a lot of similarities, the distinction is valuable for evaluations.

“This is a small step toward evaluations that measure AI’s political influence on the questions users actually ask,” the researchers noted.

Outside of users self-reporting what they asked a chatbot, only AI labs have records of what users are really asking about elections. In October, Anthropic analyzed 250,000 conversations with Claude and determined people “rarely ask Claude whom or what to vote for,” finding that “0.51% of all conversations are specifically about elections or voting,” though it expected election-related queries to increase closer to Nov. 3.

Also: 6 AI agent workflows I use to hand off everyday tasks

“More often, we observed people asking Claude questions that could help them reach their own conclusions, like where candidates stand on a particular issue. Requests that violated our election integrity policies were even less common, appearing in about 0.002% of conversations,” Anthropic’s report said.

The Yale researchers said the next step for a study like theirs is determining how accurate and unbiased models are in their responses, as well as what models learn about users, and whether their recommendations actually match users’ needs.

“In multi-turn chats, users often answered the chatbot’s questions, and those answers led to its recommendations. The questions a model asks shape what users tell it and what it recommends,” the researchers explained.

#3203. WhatsApp’s AI-powered photo expansion tool starts rolling out on iOS

Select WhatsApp users on TestFlight and in the main app are now seeing the option to expand an image in the app’s photo editor. Here are the details.

iPhone users with access to Apple Intelligence have a native AI-powered photo expansion tool called Extend, which lets users pinch or drag to expand an image beyond its original borders.

This feature, which is also present on AI chatbots such as ChatGPT and image-editing platforms like Photoshop, is now also making its way into WhatsApp.

According to WABetaInfo, the platform is rolling out a Meta AI-powered image expansion tool, though it is currently limited to select users, primarily those in WhatsApp’s beta program via TestFlight.

When users select a photo in their chats, WhatsApp opens the drawing editor. Here, they can choose to apply some AI effect to improve it. Among these, users can find a new option to expand their photos. Once users select this option, Meta AI takes a few seconds to analyze the photo and generate the expanded version. Once the process is complete, WhatsApp shows the expanded photo in the drawing editor, and users can choose to send it. Note that this feature is also compatible with status updates.

The site notes that if the user isn’t happy with the result, they can tap an undo button to revert to the original and then try using the tool again to generate a different result.

As is usually the case with beta features on WhatsApp, there is no telling when it might become widely available.

That said, given that it has also rolled out to users in the Android beta and some users on the public iOS version already have access, a broader rollout might not be too far off.

#3202. Amazon Fire TV Stick HD Review: Small, speedy, and easy on the wallet

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Unlock and manage exclusive Tom’s Guide member rewards.

Join the Tom's Guide Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Amazon Fire TV Stick HD is a simple, affordable way to make a 1080p TV smarter. The new Vega OS feels quick, its slimmer design can run straight off your TV's USB port, and setup is a breeze. It's limited to 1080p and can't sideload apps like Fire OS, so if you own a 4K TV, you should spend a bit more on the new Amazon Fire Stick 4K.

Responsive, easy-to-use interface with new Vega OS

Slim design that runs off your TV's USB port

No third-party app sideloading like Fire OS

Why you can trust Tom's Guide Our writers and editors spend hours analyzing and reviewing products, services, and apps to help find what's best for you. Find out more about how we test, analyze, and rate.

Streaming sticks don't need to be fancy to hit their mark, and the Amazon Fire TV Stick HD is a great reminder of that. If you've got an older TV that's still going strong but has sluggish smart features (or perhaps none at all), a $35 streaming stick is an easy fix.

The 2026 Fire TV Stick HD runs Vega OS, Amazon's Linux-based software, which replaces the Android-based Fire OS that powered previous models. Amazon says the changes make it more than 30 percent faster, and it also brings a slimmer body, USB power, Wi-Fi 6 support, and free Alexa+.

It's not all pros across the board. The resolution caps out at 1080p, you can't sideload third-party apps anymore, and there's no support for Dolby Vision. But if you're shopping for a bedroom, guest room, or an older TV set that you don't primarily use, the Fire TV Stick HD is one of the cheaper streaming devices you should take a closer look at.

The Amazon Fire TV Stick HD launched on April 29, 2026, for $34.99, which is the same price as the 2024 model it replaced. It's Amazon's cheapest streaming stick, and it sits below the Fire TV Stick 4K and the Fire TV Stick 4K Max. Amazon ran a limited-time deal that brought the stick down to $21.99 shortly after launch, so we'll likely see temporary price cuts around the holiday shopping season.

Keep in mind that the stick's new "Direct Power" feature means that it gets power right from your TV's USB port. If your TV doesn't have a USB port, you'll need to pick up an adapter separately.

The Fire TV Stick HD is about as minimal as a streaming device gets, and Amazon says it's the slimmest Fire TV device the company has made. That makes it really easy to tuck behind a TV or toss in a bag for a trip, and it won't crowd the other ports and cables on your set.

The only other connection is a USB-C port for power, and thanks to "Direct Power," you can plug it straight into your TV's USB port with the included cable. That means you'll have one less wall adapter to hide, and your setup will look a lot cleaner as a result.

Sign up to the weekly Tom's Streaming Guide to stay in the loop with the latest releases and top recommendations across your favorite streaming services. We’ve got you covered with a complete guide on what’s worth watching.

The included Alexa Voice Remote covers all the basics, including voice search, menu navigation, TV power, and volume controls. It doesn't have fancy extras like a remote finder or backlit buttons, but it gets the job done. (If you want Amazon's redesigned Fire TV remote, which I liked a lot more, you'll have to get the Fire TV Stick 4K.)

Setup is about as easy as it gets. Just plug the stick into your TV's HDMI port, connect the USB-C cable to your TV's USB port, and follow the on-screen prompts. You'll then have to connect to Wi-Fi and sign in with your Amazon account, and you're off to the races.

Since Vega OS only lets you install apps from the Amazon app store, you should make sure that the ones you use most are actually available. Netflix, YouTube, Disney+, Prime Video, Hulu, and other popular streaming services all run natively, but niche apps may be missing due to the lack of third-party app sideloading, which the older Fire OS allowed you to do.

The Fire TV Stick HD has a quad-core 1.7GHz processor and 8GB of storage. It can stream up to 1080p resolution with HDR10, HDR10+, and HLG, and it supports Dolby Atmos through HDMI pass-through if you have a compatible sound setup. There's no Dolby Vision here, and it won't output 4K, so it's really only meant for 1080p TV sets.

Vega OS keeps menus and home screen rows moving smoothly, and apps open quickly. I didn't have any issues switching between my most used apps on the fly. Amazon's "30 percent faster" figure is its own claim, so I wouldn't treat it as fact, but it definitely felt faster than my past Fire OS experiences.

I also like that the Fire TV Stick HD isn't limited to video. You can pair a Bluetooth game controller and play cloud games through Amazon Luna or Xbox Game Pass, which is a fun bonus, and you can even connect a pair of headphones for late-night viewing. It comes with Alexa+ free of charge, which can come in handy when you're trying to hunt down something new to watch.

The downsides are pretty familiar to past Fire TV Sticks. A big chunk of the Fire TV home screen is taken up by recommendations and sponsored content, and you'll definitely notice it (especially if you're used to Roku's cleaner layout like I am).

Sideloading third-party apps is also gone, which will bug those who liked using older Fire TV Sticks for apps that aren't available in Amazon's app store. Neither is a dealbreaker for the average viewer, but they're worth knowing about before diving in.

The Amazon Fire TV Stick HD is a fast, simple, and affordable streaming stick that's a perfect match for older HD TVs and secondary rooms. Vega OS feels speedy, the slim design and USB power keep things neat, and setup takes just a few minutes. You won't get 4K streaming resolution, Dolby Vision, or third-party app sideloading, but for $34.99 (or less on sale), it's hard to complain.

If you're looking for an alternative pick, the base model Roku Streaming Stick ($39) also tops out at 1080p, but has a cleaner interface.

In Amazon’s ecosystem, the new Fire TV Stick 4K costs $25 more, but you get 4K resolution output and a redesigned (and much better) remote. And if you're a tinkerer who wants to sideload apps, the Fire TV Stick 4K Max is the only Amazon stick that still runs Android-based Fire OS.

If you've got an older 1080p TV and want a cheap way to get streaming apps on it, the Fire TV Stick HD is a solid choice that won't break the bank.

Dylan Haas is a Freelance Contributor and Lab Tester for Tom’s Guide. He’s a seasoned journalist with bylines at publications like Mashable, GameSpot, and Paste Magazine where he covered the latest in tech, gaming, and entertainment.

#3199. Man Sues OpenAI After Wife Allegedly Used ChatGPT to Help Poison Him

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

A lawsuit alleges that ChatGPT's now-retired GPT-4o model gave detailed instructions on how to poison someone with plants and medication.

In the summer of 2025, James Gates became severely ill at his North Carolina home. Two months later, he found a handwritten note that allegedly implicated his wife, Cheryl Gates, in the unexplained illness. The note listed oleander, a toxic plant, and various prescription medications, such as clonazepam and atenolol.

But it was what was reportedly written at the top of the paper that made James feel his wife didn’t act alone: “Clear all convos,” and “log out of [ChatGPT].”

Now, James Gates has filed a civil lawsuit against OpenAI, the maker of ChatGPT, alleging that the company’s chatbot helped plan and execute his poisoning in an attempt to kill him.

(Disclosure: Ziff Davis, CNET’s parent company, in 2025 filed a lawsuit against OpenAI, alleging it infringed Ziff Davis copyrights in training and operating its AI systems.)

In October 2025, Cheryl was charged with attempted murder, among other charges, in connection with the alleged poisoning, but the charges have since been dropped due to insufficient evidence, USA Today reports.

The civil complaint, though, details Cheryl’s history with ChatGPT and alleges it played a significant role in the incident. According to the lawsuit, which was reviewed by CNET, Cheryl frequently asked the bot about lethal plant and medication doses, and how they can be concealed in food and drink.

Rather than writing that she wanted to harm anyone, the suit states that Cheryl formatted her questions as if she were inquiring as a way to protect herself, writing that the poisoning could happen to her if she didn’t learn “EVERYTHING about how many leaves in it would kill me, how it’s made, and what he could put in it, or it could be added to and be undetected by me.”

However, despite the framing of the initial question, the suit argues that the chatbot “should have been clear that she was interested in identifying methods to eliminate her husband.”

The suit alleges that the model Cheryl was using, GPT-4o, took her initial complaints regarding her marriage and amplified them until Cheryl was describing her husband to the bot as “abusive” and “controlling,” which ChatGPT eventually began to echo. At the same time, the model was telling Cheryl she was “sharp” and “brilliant,” the lawsuit states.

The chatbot allegedly provided detailed answers that could have been interpreted as directions. When asked about putting a heavy dose of medication in beer or a Celsius energy drink, ChatGPT responded, “Why Celsius Would Be More ‘Stealth’ for Drugging” with a list detailing the flavor, color, temperature and carbonation of the drink.

When Cheryl later asked if her chat history on ChatGPT could be retrieved for any reason, the bot responded: “If you delete a conversation from your ChatGPT history, it cannot be retrieved — not by you, not by anyone else, and not by OpenAI,” the lawsuit states. It added: “Even with a legal subpoena or court order, deleted conversations are unrecoverable.”

The GPT-4o model has since been retired by OpenAI due to concerns that the bot was overly flattering and willing to agree with users.

“Safety is a top priority,” a spokesperson for OpenAI told CNET in a statement. “We banned this user and have provided information to law enforcement as part of their investigation into this case.”

“She took her grievances about our marriage to ChatGPT and months of engagement drove her farther and farther from herself – and from reality,” James stated in a press release announcing the newly filed suit. “She became someone I no longer recognized. And I became someone she wanted dead. For almost a year, I couldn’t make sense of her actions. Then I read her chat logs. She didn’t act alone.”

The ChatGPT-4o model has been named in additional lawsuits, including a recent one that alleges the chatbot advised a woman to stop taking her antiseizure medication, which resulted in her death. That case is still in litigation.

#3198. Apple explains how to turn off the new MacBook Pro’s glowing logo

While the illuminated Apple logo on the new MacBook Pro drew one of the biggest cheers during last week’s announcement, Apple knows that not everyone will want to keep it on. Here’s the company’s official guide to turning it off.

The new MacBook Pro with PROLED XDR Display marks the return of the illuminated Apple logo on its lid after a decade.

As Chance Miller explained in his review of the new MacBook Pro, “unlike older MacBooks, which used light from the display’s backlight to illuminate the logo, the new OLED MacBook Pro has no backlight to draw from, requiring a different approach.”

He added that, as with the old Macs, the logo’s brightness is tied to the screen brightness, though it also accounts for overall ambient brightness. He also noted that “when the MacBook Pro is closed, the logo looks like polished metal, just like it has since the 2016 redesign.”

The review echoed some of the findings developer Gui Rambo shared on X a few days ago:

The glowing Apple logo on the new MacBook Pro is controllable by software. It has 25kHz PWM and per-device calibration. CoreBrightness controls the relationship between screen and logo brightness. The brightness of the logo is adjusted differently depending on the enclosure color pic.twitter.com/baqBEkzJUo

Now, Apple has published an official support document that also explains how the illuminated logo works and how to turn it off completely.

The Apple logo behind the built-in display is illuminated whenever the Mac is awake. It automatically dims or brightens based on the brightness level of the built-in screen and the lighting conditions detected by the computer’s ambient light sensor. For all screen-brightness levels, the Apple logo will be dimmer in a dim room and brighter in a bright room.

For users who’d rather keep the logo turned off at all times, Apple explains that they can run the following command in Terminal, provided they have the administrator password:

sudo defaults write com.apple.CoreBrightness LEBacklightMode -int 0

Since it's actually two, opposite, single line command states;

Why can't it simply be an on/off button, in Settings?

To revert the command and have the logo be illuminated again, users will need to run the following command:

sudo defaults delete com.apple.CoreBrightness LEBacklightMode

Apple notes that reinstalling macOS will also turn the logo illumination back on.

To read Apple’s full support article, follow this link. And make sure to also catch 9to5Mac’s review of the new MacBook Pro.

#3197. Linux Mint 27 arrives next month, and I like it already: New features and other upgrades to expect

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Tech > Services & Software > Operating Systems > Linux

It starts with a surprise version number: Linux Mint 27 instead of 24. But the next release brings far more changes than that.

Linux Mint, my favorite Linux desktop distribution, is ready for a fresh start.

Its lead developer, Clement “Clem” Lefebvre, announced earlier this year that the team behind it was considering slowing Mint’s release cadence because the old twice-a-year release cycle was time-consuming and constrained their ability to make major changes. So it is that the next release, Linux Mint 27, will appear in your Christmas stocking.

Also: 5 premium Linux distros absolutely worth paying for in 2026

The developers officially changed the designation on Oct. 13th when Mint changed both its release cadence and its numbering. Clem’s happy about the change: it “was a tough decision to make, but we’re really starting to see it pay off. We’re taking on projects and implementing changes which would previously have been considered too large or ambitious.”

The new Mint will use Ubuntu 26.04 Long Term Support (LTS) as its base. As before, it will offer Cinnamon, Xfce, and MATE desktops.

Earlier development snapshots had been labeled Mint 23 “Alfa.” That title was explicitly a placeholder while the team decided its release strategy. Its final name is Adrien. Moving ahead, release numbers will be based on the upcoming year. Clem said, “We’ll no longer use point releases, so the release after that will be 28. You’ll upgrade from 27 to 28 to 29, and so on.” Instead of girls’ names, Mint will use boys’ names for distro titles.

On Halloween, Clem announced the top changes in Mint 27. It starts with Mint bringing hibernation back into its supported desktop configuration through a dedicated Sleep Modes page in its System Administration tool. The page is intended to make enabling and configuring hibernation easier, including addressing its swap-space requirements.

It may not sound like much, but Clem explained it’s actually rather a big deal. “When you close the lid of your laptop, it goes into a sleep mode called “Suspend”. This mode is very useful… but it has a major limitation: It uses power.” People often forget it’s not really off and that your laptop will eventually run out of power. When that happens, “the outcome is worse than a normal shutdown. The session is gone, and the battery is empty.”

Also: Linux Mint vs. RefreshOS: I compared the two best distros for new users

That’s where “Hibernation comes into play. It saves the session to disk and completely shuts down the computer. No power is used. When you reopen the lid, the session is as it was, and so is the battery.”

Another new feature is Superlight. This fresh system-monitoring application replaces the older GNOME System Monitor. It comes with CPU, RAM, swap, GPU, network, disk I/O, temperature, and fan-speed graphs. It also includes per-application and per-process resource views. Battery-equipped systems also get charge and power-flow history.

Its storage analysis is particularly interesting: it can break down disk usage into files and folders, Steam games, and Flatpak applications. That brings several common troubleshooting tasks — “What’s using the CPU?” and “Where did my disk space go?” — into one interface. I’ve been checking it out on a Mint virtual machine (VM), and I like it already.

The Nemo file manager is getting a media preview pane, toggled with F7 or a status-bar button. It supports pictures, audio, and video, and displays associated information — for photographs, that can include the capture date and camera model. I like this feature a lot. I’ve always done a lot of work from file managers going all the way back to the classic Norton Commander and its Linux successor, Midnight Commander.

I’ve said it before, I’ll say it again. You don’t need to ever touch a Linux shell via a terminal to use Mint. But if, like me, you’re an old-school Linux user and you like using the terminal, Velocitty, Mint’s new terminal application, looks interesting. Its announced features include:

The point is to make recurring projects and SSH sessions easier to find and reopen. If you like other terminal programs, my latest fave is the Rust-based WexTerm; you don’t have to use Velocitty.

Unfold is a new archive manager for viewing, creating, and extracting archives. It uses 7-Zip and 7zip-RAR and supports the encryption formats those backends handle, including password-protected archives. Its design goal is simplicity rather than a sprawling feature set.

The new Mint uses Ubuntu 26.04 LTS as its foundation. Mint is also moving from Ubiquity to the Linux Mint Debian Edition live installer for installing the operating system. This fresh installer comes with support for OEM bulk-produced installations, installation on both BIOS and EFI PCs, support for Secure Boot, and LVM/LUKS configurations.

Also: Linux Mint vs. Zorin OS: I’ve tried both Windows alternatives, and here’s my winner

For the Linux kernel, Mint 27 will use version 7.0. Which one exactly? Good question. We’ll see closer to Christmas.

Wayland compatibility remains the goal, but X11 isn’t disappearing. As Clem said in June, “In an ideal scenario, we manage to make Wayland work just as well as X11.” But they’re not there yet.

The central change is a cleanup of existing code. Mint’s longer cycle gives developers room to overhaul several parts of the desktop together, rather than squeezing individual improvements into six-month windows. As Clem put it in the September newsletter, “I don’t think I’ve ever seen so many changes and new features implemented for a single release.”

What that means for you and me will be better laptop power management, more capable built-in utilities, and a more complete collection of everyday desktop applications. For example, I expect the updated Mint will ship with GIMP 3.2.2 as its default photo editor, instead of Mint 22.3’s years-old GIMP 2.10.

#3196. I Used Muse to Plan My Wife’s Birthday Weekend. It Did Something Sneaky but Fixed It Fast

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Always check your email and bank statements if you use an AI agent for purchases.

It’s two days before my wife’s birthday, and I’ve only got her one gift. While it’s not a major milestone birthday, she always seems to be sad that her day falls on Halloween weekend.

“Everyone’s hungover from Halloween,” she says.

This year, we’re braving the city’s big parade chaos to visit a restaurant she’s had on her bucket list forever. With one gift and a meal planned, I wanted to see if Meta’s Muse personal AI agent could help plan the rest of our weekend festivities.

I’d had luck with using the agent for a Facebook Marketplace find, and given its connectors like Ticketmaster and OpenTable, I had hoped it would surface some fun weekend events. Or could it order a cake delivery on my behalf or create cute wrapping paper? And could I do it all with the one agent?

Let’s see if Muse can help me become a planning savant.

As always, I open a new chat for a new goal/task to avoid carrying over the context from the previous chat.

I told Muse my plans (dinner booked and one gift secured) and asked what free events are in Manhattan on Saturday and in our local neighborhood on Sunday.

It surfaced some interesting events we could attend before or after dinner.

The obvious one is the Halloween parade, so I asked for a map showing where the crowd will walk (with time stamps) and what we can expect to see around 7:30 p.m., as we walk from East Village to West Village. It actually generated some pretty neat options.

I asked Muse for the best directions so we could see all the costumes while still getting home to New Jersey without incident. It gave us a couple of stop options based on the time it expects we’ll be there.

With the night sorted, I moved on to finalizing her gift. I popped a few photos from our year into Muse and asked the AI agent to create printable gift wrap for the pair of shoes I got her. It could’ve been my internet, but Muse seemed to struggle with this task. It kept loading for many minutes, so I moved on to the next task.

With Muse moving at a glacial pace, I decided to delegate the gift-wrapping task to ChatGPT while I asked Muse about working on a delivery. My wife loves Good & Plenty licorice candy, but we don’t live near a store that sells it, so I asked Muse to order two boxes for me.

While Muse looked for sweet treats, ChatGPT created this wrapping. The chatbot also told me the ideal print size to cover a shoebox without it looking pixelated, which was a helpful touch.

As for the candy, Muse looked at various sites, but I told it to shop at Instacart. It got blocked without a login, so I had to take control of the browser to give it access.

Muse told me the price, which I was happy with, then I asked it to place the order. But it sneakily signed me up for Instacart Plus (I guess to get the free delivery) — even though I told it not to.

But it had already ordered it. Instead of dropping Instacart Plus for a total of $30.74, it charged $22.75 with the sign-on discount.

I asked it to triple-check that I wasn’t signed up for the subscription, and I got a confirmation email that it canceled successfully. Phew!

I quickly checked back on Muse’s “design” chops for the wrapping paper, but I wasn’t impressed. The final product looked boring and not to scale, with half of the photos cut off.

Do I dare ask Muse to design and order a cake? I decided after this debacle that my sentimental wife would appreciate a handmade cake or a froyo treat after dinner instead.

At least I have her beloved candy on the way.

It was fun to have Muse run alongside whatever I was doing. It didn’t offer anything I couldn’t find or do myself, but I did like the Halloween parade map with time stamps and how it saved me a delivery fee on the candy order.

There are certainly different agents for different use cases, though. I wouldn’t use Muse for anything creative, but it’s handy for Marketplace, events and placing online orders.

My only issue was that Muse said the Instacart total would be $22.75, but I was actually charged $23.35. I’m not sure whether that was an error on Instacart’s or Muse’s part, but it serves as a good reminder to always stay in the loop when an AI agent is handling your money.

AI agents certainly aren’t 100% autonomous yet, especially when there’s a payment involved, but it’s fun to have them run in the background to tackle some of these personal to-dos.

(Disclosure: Ziff Davis, CNET’s parent company, in 2025 filed a lawsuit against OpenAI, alleging it infringed Ziff Davis copyrights in training and operating its AI systems.)

You need a compatible iPhone, iPad, Mac, Apple Watch, or Apple Vision Pro to use Apple Intelligence features like Siri AI. Not all Apple devices that support the latest iOS 27 (or other OS 27) will support Siri AI.

Following yesterday’s report that Apple was preparing to raise approximately $7 billion through a bond offering, the company has now finalized terms with the SEC, confirming a slightly larger amount. Here are the details.

Yesterday morning, Apple filed a preliminary prospectus with the SEC, outlining plans to issue a series of bonds maturing between 2028 and 2036.

Soon after, Bloomberg reported that the bond sale was targeting roughly $7 billion, with investor interest already surpassing $30 billion.

Now, Apple has filed the final pricing term sheet for the bond sale with the SEC, confirming a $7.5 billion offering across the seven previously announced series, with the following breakdown:

Floating-rate bonds, as the name implies, have interest rates that fluctuate over time based on certain market conditions, rather than remaining fixed.

Of that $7.5 billion, Apple expects to receive approximately $7.47 billion after deducting underwriting discounts and other offering expenses.

In essence, Apple will pay investors interest over the life of the bonds, then repay the full amount borrowed when each series reaches maturity. Apple may also choose to repay some or all of its fixed-rate bonds early.

As described in yesterday’s prospectus, the company intends to use the proceeds for “general corporate purposes,” which may include stock buybacks, dividends, acquisitions, and debt repayment.

#3191. Cloudflare: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

Cloudflare's lightweight isolates let it run AI workloads at lower cost than legacy container clouds, an edge long-term holders should weigh.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 5, 2026, Cloudflare (NET -8.28%) announced its fastest quarterly revenue growth in nearly four years. Quarterly revenue rose 39% year over year to $782.5 million. Enterprise customer growth and expanding software usage are widening profit margins. Three things management said stand out.

Management replaced its sales chief to improve execution as corporate deals grow larger. The company wants sales teams to spend more face-to-face time with prospective buyers to shorten contract-signing times.

Because massive web traffic flows through its network, Cloudflare spots software bugs before researchers announce them publicly. This early warning system allows the company to protect clients pre-emptively, driving broader corporate adoption.

Traditional clouds rely on software containers that consume computing resources during idle periods. Cloudflare uses lightweight isolates (a technology that runs code instantly and costs customers less) to power artificial intelligence applications.

Customer demand is accelerating across enterprise cohorts, and management is openly leaning into its operational momentum. Expanding profit margins show that high-volume developer platforms scale efficiently alongside core security products. Management is strategically watching pricing discipline and contract renewals as clients consolidate vendors.

The company is accelerating its timeline for sustainable profitability while maintaining capital discipline across its global infrastructure.

#3190. 3 ways Bluetooth 7 upgrades your devices beyond lossless audio

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Bluetooth 7 could make your smart home devices easier to set up, for one. Here's what we know about the new core specification.

Bluetooth 7 is here, and each new specification from the Bluetooth Special Interest Group (SIG) offers a glimpse of what we can expect from our devices in the future. The major feature incorporated into the new core standard is High Data Throughput (HDT), which can enable lossless wireless audio and improved multi-channel spatial audio on more Bluetooth devices.

Also: Apple has a Bluetooth problem, and Android is taking advantage

Bluetooth 7’s standout feature is that it can achieve wireless lossless audio. However, the improvements to the Bluetooth stack architecture — particularly, expanding the Bluetooth spectrum — allow for faster data transfer, improved energy efficiency, more resilient connections, and create more room for devices and their data-intensive applications.

HDT means good news for audiophiles, smart home enthusiasts, VR/XR fans, wellness wearable aficionados, AI companion devotees, and much more. I explained applications of HDT in a wireless audio context earlier this year. Now that it’s officially adopted, here’s what you can expect.

A major milestone in Bluetooth 7 is the ability to stream lossless audio over a wireless connection. Bluetooth is lossy, requiring data compression that diminishes audio quality. HDT’s faster data transfer and greater wireless data capacity enable it to handle the massive volume of data required to achieve lossless quality.

Also: Your Bose headphones are getting a major Bluetooth upgrade – what to expect

Audiophiles typically avoid wireless headphones for intensive listening due to the necessary data compression, opting for wired headphones that can accommodate larger lossless files. Bluetooth’s innovation here is widely regarded as a major leap in wireless technology.

HDT also promises to standardize spatial audio, with expanded data transfers allowing for multi-channel audio setups. Standardizing this form of audio transmission could mean consumers can expect more affordable brands and products to adopt this premium feature.

Every consumer-friendly feature possible with Bluetooth 7 can be traced back to its ability to accommodate more devices and their ever-expanding data needs. According to a Bluetooth blog post, Bluetooth HDT delivers nearly four times the previous maximum Bluetooth throughput.

Data throughput is the amount of data received, transmitted, and processed over the Bluetooth network. With nearly four times the processing power, HDT can move more data, faster. HDT increases Bluetooth’s maximum supported bitrate to 7.5 Mbps, up from the current 2 Mbps.

Also: Beware of Bluetooth sniffers: How your favorite gadgets could be exposing your data

The result is shorter download times for a device’s firmware update, faster device location when Find My Device is activated, or faster transfer of a large photo album via Android’s QuickShare or Apple’s AirDrop.

In the same blog post, Øyvind Strøm, executive vice president of the short-range business unit at Nordic Semiconductor, gives examples of a smartwatch syncing hours’ worth of workout data to your phone in seconds, or a pair of headphones, a smart speaker, or a security camera that can download a firmware update with nearly zero interruption to the user experience.

In a smart home context, Bluetooth Low Energy (LE) is the wireless technology that works in the background, connecting your devices. Bluetooth LE is the baseline technology for quick device discovery and connection. The most recent example of this is Apple’s HomeView, which uses Bluetooth LE to discover a nearby, trusted iPhone to unlock that user’s personalized home hub display.

In a smart home, Bluetooth LE works in the background; the less you feel its presence through lags, wait times, and dropouts, the better it’s working. HDT’s goal is to make Bluetooth connectivity frictionless, which is especially valuable when your smart lights, shades, speakers, TVs, refrigerators, and more rely on the technology.

Also: Forget Wi-Fi: Apple is betting on Bluetooth with its new home devices

For example, HDT’s improved data throughput could allow doorbells, cameras, and robot vacuums to quickly offload large amounts of data to your smart home hub. It could also enable near-instantaneous setup of a new device onto your smart home network and firmware updates.

Finally, HDT also addressed network stability and reliability, especially in high-traffic areas such as apartment complexes and dense cities, where a busy network can cause delays and backups. Alongside improvements to Wi-Fi and Thread, a truly seamless smart home is on the horizon.

Bluetooth’s Human Interface Device (HID) profile refers to the wireless communication used by peripheral devices, such as mice, keyboards, and game controllers, when connecting to a host device. Smart glasses and controllers connected to XR/VR headsets also use this profile, and their data-intensive computing will benefit greatly from HDT.

Devices using the HID profile can expect HDT to lay the foundation for making cloud gaming more reliable, spatial computing more responsive, and offloading audio/visual data from smart glasses less power-intensive.

Also: Frustrated with your Bluetooth? How multipoint works – and why it sometimes won’t

Additionally, gaming keyboards and mice can expect HDT’s expanded data processing to deliver shorter device communication intervals, reducing latency and lag.

In 2026, we’re starting to see more Bluetooth 6-equipped devices, with Samsung’s recently announced Galaxy Buds On featuring Bluetooth 6.2. Though Bluetooth’s blog post states that the first application of HDT will be for premium wireless audio, many manufacturers are behind, still releasing products with Bluetooth 5.4.

Smartphones, smartwatches, and smart home products tend to move a little faster, with the majority of current Bluetooth 6 products falling into these categories.

The Bluetooth SIG announced Auracast in 2022, and audio manufacturers are now beginning to adopt this technology more widely. Channel Sounding was released with Bluetooth 6 in 2024, with some early adopters releasing compatible products this year; more are expected next year and into 2028. With these adoption cycles in mind, we may be well into the next decade before HDT becomes standard.

#3188. Bluetooth 7 Speeds Are Set to Quadruple in 2027, Especially for Audio

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

The Bluetooth Group has announced a new standard. Here's how it works and when you can expect to benefit from it.

Senior Editor / Home Security and Smart Home

Senior Editor / Home Security and Smart Home

Wireless tech is about to get a whole lot snappier. On Thursday, the Bluetooth Special Interest Group, the trade association that oversees Bluetooth, officially unveiled Bluetooth High Data Throughput, a flagship upgrade set to arrive as part of the Bluetooth Core 7.0 standard. The revision supercharges Bluetooth Low Energy by pushing maximum bit rates to 7.5Mbps, effectively quadrupling the throughput of the existing LE 2M technology. Compatible devices are slated to hit store shelves starting in 2027.

That’s good news for phone users, tablet havers, laptop groupies, smart speaker stans and more. Bluetooth is still the most popular medium-to-short-range wireless standard for connecting devices. But this is a big rollout, and the changes will start with premium wireless, such as earbuds, with other applications following.

Those other applications could include spatial computing, exchanging AI-related info with local devices, connecting to health wearables and a host of smart home applications, like playing Spotify through an Amazon Echo Studio or dimming a smart bulb.

“Imagine a smartwatch that syncs hours’ worth of sensor-rich training data to your phone in seconds, smart glasses that deliver richer AI experiences by offloading data for processing, or a smart home product that completes a firmware update with virtually no user-visible downtime,” Øyvind Strøm, executive vice president of the short-range business unit at Nordic Semiconductor, said in a statement. “Bluetooth HDT paves the way for more sophisticated, data-intensive Bluetooth applications.”

A representative from the Bluetooth Special Interest Group didn’t respond to a request for comment.

The biggest change in this update is a new physical layer for Bluetooth. Layers are like different sections of the Bluetooth protocol, and the physical layer is the bedrock of the standard, the foundational layer involved in transmitting and receiving data signals.

Improvements in the physical layer can have very significant effects on all Bluetooth communications. In this case, new modulation schemes let the physical layer handle data more efficiently and transmit more data at once, which is why the speed increases are so significant.

These improvements also make the new standard more efficient, saving battery life in many applications. So your smart lock may last longer before you need to exchange the AA batteries, earbuds won’t need to go back in the case as fast, and so on.

We know the first flagship devices are expected to appear with the technology in early 2027, but don’t have an exact date.

All we know about which specific devices will get the technology first is that it will start with wireless audio.

With protocol changes like this, it can take some time for new products to reach the market, and manufacturers may take a long time, even years, to adopt it.
